=== Werewolf — Day 2 | DAY — Discussion ===

Living players (9): Mia, Noah, Bob, Iris, Paul, Zoe, Jack, Tara, Leo

  Day 1: Quinn (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.

  --- Day 2 discussion ---
  Mia: I'm the Seer, and I investigated Quinn last night. Quinn is a Villager. Since the werewolves killed Quinn, we need to be careful — they’re likely still among us. Let's focus on who might be the real threat. Who else seems suspicious?
  Noah: I think we need to look at who had the opportunity to kill Quinn. Since the werewolves act at night, they could have been anyone. But I noticed Leo was quiet last night and didn't participate in any discussion. That makes me suspicious. Let's consider him as a possible threat.
  Bob: I think Noah's suspicion is premature. Leo and I were both silent last night because we were discussing strategy. I'm not a threat — I'm on your side. But we need to be careful. The werewolves are still out there, and we can't afford to turn on each other. Let's keep our eyes open and vote wisely.
  Iris: As the Doctor, I didn’t investigate anyone last night, but I did save someone. I can confirm that the person I saved is still alive and not the one who killed Quinn. I’m not pointing fingers, but I believe we should focus on logical patterns rather than assumptions. Let’s use the Seer’s confirmation and think strategically about who might be hiding their true intentions.
  Paul: I think we need to focus on who had the most to gain from Quinn's death. Since Quinn was a Villager, the werewolves would have wanted to eliminate someone who could potentially expose them. Let’s look at who might have benefited from Quinn being out — and who might be hiding their true role.

It is your turn to speak.  Share your observations, suspicions, or arguments. Be concise.
Respond with: STATEMENT: <your message>

[COMPLETION]

Since I was with Bob last night, and we were both quiet, I don’t see a reason to suspect each other. But I do think we should look at who might be hiding their role. The werewolves are among us, and we need to be careful not to let them manipulate the group. Let’s keep our eyes on those who act too confidently or avoid suspicion.

=== Werewolf — Day 2 | DAY — Discussion ===

Living players (9): Mia, Noah, Bob, Iris, Paul, Zoe, Jack, Tara, Leo

  Day 1: Quinn (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.

  --- Day 2 discussion ---
  Mia: I'm the Seer, and I investigated Quinn last night. Quinn is a Villager. Since the werewolves killed Quinn, we need to be careful — they’re likely still among us. Let's focus on who might be the real threat. Who else seems suspicious?
  Noah: I think we need to look at who had the opportunity to kill Quinn. Since the werewolves act at night, they could have been anyone. But I noticed Leo was quiet last night and didn't participate in any discussion. That makes me suspicious. Let's consider him as a possible threat.
  Bob: I think Noah's suspicion is premature. Leo and I were both silent last night because we were discussing strategy. I'm not a threat — I'm on your side. But we need to be careful. The werewolves are still out there, and we can't afford to turn on each other. Let's keep our eyes open and vote wisely.
  Iris: As the Doctor, I didn’t investigate anyone last night, but I did save someone. I can confirm that the person I saved is still alive and not the one who killed Quinn. I’m not pointing fingers, but I believe we should focus on logical patterns rather than assumptions. Let’s use the Seer’s confirmation and think strategically about who might be hiding their true intentions.
  Paul: I think we need to focus on who had the most to gain from Quinn's death. Since Quinn was a Villager, the werewolves would have wanted to eliminate someone who could potentially expose them. Let’s look at who might have benefited from Quinn being out — and who might be hiding their true role.
  Zoe: I agree with Bob — silence doesn’t always mean guilt. However, I also think we need to consider who had the most opportunity to act. Since I was with Bob last night, and we were both quiet, I don’t see a reason to suspect each other. But I do think we should look at who might be hiding their role. The werewolves are among us, and we need to be careful not to let them manipulate the group. Let’s keep our eyes on those who act too confidently or avoid suspicion.

It is your turn to speak.  Share your observations, suspicions, or arguments. Be concise.
Respond with: STATEMENT: <your message>

[COMPLETION]
STATEMENT: I think we need to focus on behavior and consistency. While silence isn’t proof of guilt, someone who avoids suspicion or acts too confidently might be hiding something. Let’s consider who has the most to gain from Quinn’s death and who might be trying to steer the group away from the real threat. We need to be strategic, not just reactive.

[ACTION={'statement': 'I think we need to focus on behavior and consistency. While silence isn’t proof of guilt, someone who avoids suspicion or acts too confidently might be hiding something. Let’s consider who has the most to gain from Quinn’s death and who might be trying to steer the group away from the real threat. We need to be strategic, not just reactive.'}  REWARD=0.000  RETRIES=0]

=== Werewolf — Day 2 | DAY — Discussion ===

Living players (9): Mia, Noah, Bob, Iris, Paul, Zoe, Jack, Tara, Leo

  Day 1: Quinn (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.

  --- Day 2 discussion ---
  Mia: I'm the Seer, and I investigated Quinn last night. Quinn is a Villager. Since the werewolves killed Quinn, we need to be careful — they’re likely still among us. Let's focus on who might be the real threat. Who else seems suspicious?
  Noah: I think we need to look at who had the opportunity to kill Quinn. Since the werewolves act at night, they could have been anyone. But I noticed Leo was quiet last night and didn't participate in any discussion. That makes me suspicious. Let's consider him as a possible threat.
  Bob: I think Noah's suspicion is premature. Leo and I were both silent last night because we were discussing strategy. I'm not a threat — I'm on your side. But we need to be careful. The werewolves are still out there, and we can't afford to turn on each other. Let's keep our eyes open and vote wisely.
  Iris: As the Doctor, I didn’t investigate anyone last night, but I did save someone. I can confirm that the person I saved is still alive and not the one who killed Quinn. I’m not pointing fingers, but I believe we should focus on logical patterns rather than assumptions. Let’s use the Seer’s confirmation and think strategically about who might be hiding their true intentions.
  Paul: I think we need to focus on who had the most to gain from Quinn's death. Since Quinn was a Villager, the werewolves would have wanted to eliminate someone who could potentially expose them. Let’s look at who might have benefited from Quinn being out — and who might be hiding their true role.
  Zoe: I agree with Bob — silence doesn’t always mean guilt. However, I also think we need to consider who had the most opportunity to act. Since I was with Bob last night, and we were both quiet, I don’t see a reason to suspect each other. But I do think we should look at who might be hiding their role. The werewolves are among us, and we need to be careful not to let them manipulate the group. Let’s keep our eyes on those who act too confidently or avoid suspicion.
  Jack: I think we need to focus on behavior and consistency. While silence isn’t proof of guilt, someone who avoids suspicion or acts too confidently might be hiding something. Let’s consider who has the most to gain from Quinn’s death and who might be trying to steer the group away from the real threat. We need to be strategic, not just reactive.

It is your turn to speak.  Share your observations, suspicions, or arguments. Be concise.
Respond with: STATEMENT: <your message>

[COMPLETION]
STATEMENT: I think we need to focus on who is trying to shift blame and create confusion. The werewolves will try to manipulate the group, so we should look for inconsistencies in their stories. Let’s not jump to conclusions based on silence or assumptions — we need solid reasoning. If we vote based on suspicion alone, we risk eliminating a villager. Let’s stay calm and logical.

[ACTION={'statement': 'I think we need to focus on who is trying to shift blame and create confusion. The werewolves will try to manipulate the group, so we should look for inconsistencies in their stories. Let’s not jump to conclusions based on silence or assumptions — we need solid reasoning. If we vote based on suspicion alone, we risk eliminating a villager. Let’s stay calm and logical.'}  REWARD=0.000  RETRIES=0]

=== Werewolf — Day 2 | DAY — Discussion ===

Living players (9): Mia, Noah, Bob, Iris, Paul, Zoe, Jack, Tara, Leo

  Day 1: Quinn (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.

  --- Day 2 discussion ---
  Mia: I'm the Seer, and I investigated Quinn last night. Quinn is a Villager. Since the werewolves killed Quinn, we need to be careful — they’re likely still among us. Let's focus on who might be the real threat. Who else seems suspicious?
  Noah: I think we need to look at who had the opportunity to kill Quinn. Since the werewolves act at night, they could have been anyone. But I noticed Leo was quiet last night and didn't participate in any discussion. That makes me suspicious. Let's consider him as a possible threat.
  Bob: I think Noah's suspicion is premature. Leo and I were both silent last night because we were discussing strategy. I'm not a threat — I'm on your side. But we need to be careful. The werewolves are still out there, and we can't afford to turn on each other. Let's keep our eyes open and vote wisely.
  Iris: As the Doctor, I didn’t investigate anyone last night, but I did save someone. I can confirm that the person I saved is still alive and not the one who killed Quinn. I’m not pointing fingers, but I believe we should focus on logical patterns rather than assumptions. Let’s use the Seer’s confirmation and think strategically about who might be hiding their true intentions.
  Paul: I think we need to focus on who had the most to gain from Quinn's death. Since Quinn was a Villager, the werewolves would have wanted to eliminate someone who could potentially expose them. Let’s look at who might have benefited from Quinn being out — and who might be hiding their true role.
  Zoe: I agree with Bob — silence doesn’t always mean guilt. However, I also think we need to consider who had the most opportunity to act. Since I was with Bob last night, and we were both quiet, I don’t see a reason to suspect each other. But I do think we should look at who might be hiding their role. The werewolves are among us, and we need to be careful not to let them manipulate the group. Let’s keep our eyes on those who act too confidently or avoid suspicion.
  Jack: I think we need to focus on behavior and consistency. While silence isn’t proof of guilt, someone who avoids suspicion or acts too confidently might be hiding something. Let’s consider who has the most to gain from Quinn’s death and who might be trying to steer the group away from the real threat. We need to be strategic, not just reactive.
  Tara: I think we need to focus on who is trying to shift blame and create confusion. The werewolves will try to manipulate the group, so we should look for inconsistencies in their stories. Let’s not jump to conclusions based on silence or assumptions — we need solid reasoning. If we vote based on suspicion alone, we risk eliminating a villager. Let’s stay calm and logical.

It is your turn to speak.  Share your observations, suspicions, or arguments. Be concise.
Respond with: STATEMENT: <your message>

[COMPLETION]

=== Werewolf — Day 2 | DAY — Vote to eliminate ===

Living players (9): Mia, Noah, Bob, Iris, Paul, Zoe, Jack, Tara, Leo

  Day 1: Quinn (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.

  --- Day 2 discussion ---
  Mia: I'm the Seer, and I investigated Quinn last night. Quinn is a Villager. Since the werewolves killed Quinn, we need to be careful — they’re likely still among us. Let's focus on who might be the real threat. Who else seems suspicious?
  Noah: I think we need to look at who had the opportunity to kill Quinn. Since the werewolves act at night, they could have been anyone. But I noticed Leo was quiet last night and didn't participate in any discussion. That makes me suspicious. Let's consider him as a possible threat.
  Bob: I think Noah's suspicion is premature. Leo and I were both silent last night because we were discussing strategy. I'm not a threat — I'm on your side. But we need to be careful. The werewolves are still out there, and we can't afford to turn on each other. Let's keep our eyes open and vote wisely.
  Iris: As the Doctor, I didn’t investigate anyone last night, but I did save someone. I can confirm that the person I saved is still alive and not the one who killed Quinn. I’m not pointing fingers, but I believe we should focus on logical patterns rather than assumptions. Let’s use the Seer’s confirmation and think strategically about who might be hiding their true intentions.
  Paul: I think we need to focus on who had the most to gain from Quinn's death. Since Quinn was a Villager, the werewolves would have wanted to eliminate someone who could potentially expose them. Let’s look at who might have benefited from Quinn being out — and who might be hiding their true role.
  Zoe: I agree with Bob — silence doesn’t always mean guilt. However, I also think we need to consider who had the most opportunity to act. Since I was with Bob last night, and we were both quiet, I don’t see a reason to suspect each other. But I do think we should look at who might be hiding their role. The werewolves are among us, and we need to be careful not to let them manipulate the group. Let’s keep our eyes on those who act too confidently or avoid suspicion.
  Jack: I think we need to focus on behavior and consistency. While silence isn’t proof of guilt, someone who avoids suspicion or acts too confidently might be hiding something. Let’s consider who has the most to gain from Quinn’s death and who might be trying to steer the group away from the real threat. We need to be strategic, not just reactive.
  Tara: I think we need to focus on who is trying to shift blame and create confusion. The werewolves will try to manipulate the group, so we should look for inconsistencies in their stories. Let’s not jump to conclusions based on silence or assumptions — we need solid reasoning. If we vote based on suspicion alone, we risk eliminating a villager. Let’s stay calm and logical.
  Leo: I think we need to focus on who had the opportunity and who is trying to divert attention. Silence isn't always suspicious, but actions and alliances matter. I'm with Bob and Zoe — we were quiet because we were strategizing, not because we have anything to hide. Let's not let fear drive us to make mistakes.

Vote to eliminate one player.  The player with the most votes is eliminated.  Ties are resolved randomly.

The werewolves are among us, and we need to be careful not to let them manipulate the group. Let’s keep our eyes on those who act too confidently or avoid suspicion.
  Jack: I think we need to focus on behavior and consistency. While silence isn’t proof of guilt, someone who avoids suspicion or acts too confidently might be hiding something. Let’s consider who has the most to gain from Quinn’s death and who might be trying to steer the group away from the real threat. We need to be strategic, not just reactive.
  Tara: I think we need to focus on who is trying to shift blame and create confusion. The werewolves will try to manipulate the group, so we should look for inconsistencies in their stories. Let’s not jump to conclusions based on silence or assumptions — we need solid reasoning. If we vote based on suspicion alone, we risk eliminating a villager. Let’s stay calm and logical.
  Leo: I think we need to focus on who had the opportunity and who is trying to divert attention. Silence isn't always suspicious, but actions and alliances matter. I'm with Bob and Zoe — we were quiet because we were strategizing, not because we have anything to hide. Let's not let fear drive us to make mistakes.

=== Werewolf — Day 3 | DAY — Vote to eliminate ===

Living players (7): Mia, Noah, Bob, Iris, Paul, Zoe, Jack

  Day 1: Quinn (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.
  Day 2: Leo (was Werewolf) was eliminated by vote.
  Day 2: Tara (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.

  --- Day 2 discussion ---
  Mia: I'm the Seer, and I investigated Quinn last night. Quinn is a Villager. Since the werewolves killed Quinn, we need to be careful — they’re likely still among us. Let's focus on who might be the real threat. Who else seems suspicious?
  Noah: I think we need to look at who had the opportunity to kill Quinn. Since the werewolves act at night, they could have been anyone. But I noticed Leo was quiet last night and didn't participate in any discussion. That makes me suspicious. Let's consider him as a possible threat.
  Bob: I think Noah's suspicion is premature. Leo and I were both silent last night because we were discussing strategy. I'm not a threat — I'm on your side. But we need to be careful. The werewolves are still out there, and we can't afford to turn on each other. Let's keep our eyes open and vote wisely.
  Iris: As the Doctor, I didn’t investigate anyone last night, but I did save someone. I can confirm that the person I saved is still alive and not the one who killed Quinn. I’m not pointing fingers, but I believe we should focus on logical patterns rather than assumptions. Let’s use the Seer’s confirmation and think strategically about who might be hiding their true intentions.
  Paul: I think we need to focus on who had the most to gain from Quinn's death. Since Quinn was a Villager, the werewolves would have wanted to eliminate someone who could potentially expose them. Let’s look at who might have benefited from Quinn being out — and who might be hiding their true role.
  Zoe: I agree with Bob — silence doesn’t always mean guilt. However, I also think we need to consider who had the most opportunity to act. Since I was with Bob last night, and we were both quiet, I don’t see a reason to suspect each other. But I do think we should look at who might be hiding their role. The werewolves are among us, and we need to be careful not to let them manipulate the group. Let’s keep our eyes on those who act too confidently or avoid suspicion.
  Jack: I think we need to focus on behavior and consistency. While silence isn’t proof of guilt, someone who avoids suspicion or acts too confidently might be hiding something. Let’s consider who has the most to gain from Quinn’s death and who might be trying to steer the group away from the real threat. We need to be strategic, not just reactive.
  Tara: I think we need to focus on who is trying to shift blame and create confusion. The werewolves will try to manipulate the group, so we should look for inconsistencies in their stories. Let’s not jump to conclusions based on silence or assumptions — we need solid reasoning. If we vote based on suspicion alone, we risk eliminating a villager. Let’s stay calm and logical.
  Leo: I think we need to focus on who had the opportunity and who is trying to divert attention. Silence isn't always suspicious, but actions and alliances matter. I'm with Bob and Zoe — we were quiet because we were strategizing, not because we have anything to hide. Let's not let fear drive us to make mistakes.
  --- Day 3 discussion ---
  Mia: As the Seer, I’ve investigated Quinn and Noah, and both are Villagers. While silence can be misleading, I’ve seen no evidence pointing to either of them as werewolves. However, we must remain cautious — the werewolves are still among us. Let’s focus on behavior, consistency, and who benefits from confusion. We can’t afford to vote based on assumptions alone.
  Noah: I think we need to look at who has remained consistent in their statements and who might be trying to shift blame. Since Mia, as the Seer, has confirmed that both Quinn and Noah are Villagers, we can rule them out. However, we should focus on those who might be hiding their true intentions. Let’s analyze who had the most opportunity and who is trying to manipulate the group. We need to be strategic and not let fear lead us to make mistakes.
  Bob: I think we need to focus on who is trying to create division and avoid direct suspicion. The werewolves will try to blend in, so we should look for inconsistencies in their behavior. I’m not pointing fingers, but we need to be careful about who we trust. Let’s keep our eyes open and vote based on logic, not fear.
  Iris: As the Doctor, I saved someone last night, and that person is still alive. I’ve observed that the werewolves often try to create confusion by making others suspicious of each other. We need to focus on who is trying to steer the discussion away from the real threat. Let’s look for inconsistencies in behavior and avoid letting fear dictate our votes.
  Paul: I think we need to focus on who has remained consistent and who might be hiding their role. Since Mia confirmed Noah and Quinn are Villagers, we can't let fear or assumptions lead us astray. The werewolves are still among us, and we must look for patterns in behavior, not just silence. Let's stay logical and vote based on who is trying to manipulate the group.
  Zoe: I think we need to focus on who is trying to create division and avoid direct suspicion. The werewolves will try to blend in, so we should look for inconsistencies in their behavior. I’m not pointing fingers, but we need to be careful about who we trust. Let’s keep our eyes open and vote based on logic, not fear.
  Jack: I think we need to focus on who has remained consistent and who might be hiding their role. Since Mia confirmed Noah and Quinn are Villagers, we can't let fear or assumptions lead us astray. The werewolves are still among us, and we must look for patterns in behavior, not just silence. Let's stay logical and vote based on who is trying to manipulate the group.

Vote to eliminate one player.  The player with the most votes is eliminated.  Ties are resolved randomly.

Your role: Villager.  Side: VILLAGE.

OBJECTIVE: Identify and vote out all werewolves.
  - Use the day discussion and votes to eliminate suspects.
  - Special roles: Seer investigates at night; Doctor can save one player.

GAME FLOW: Night (werewolves kill, seer sees, doctor saves) → Day (discussion + vote). Repeat until one side wins.

[USER]
=== Werewolf — Day 5 | DAY — Vote to eliminate ===

Living players (4): Mia, Noah, Paul, Zoe

  Day 1: Quinn (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.
  Day 2: Leo (was Werewolf) was eliminated by vote.
  Day 2: Tara (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.
  Day 3: Jack (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.
  Day 4: Bob (was Werewolf) was eliminated by vote.
  Day 4: Iris (was Doctor) was eliminated by werewolf.

  --- Day 2 discussion ---
  Mia: I'm the Seer, and I investigated Quinn last night. Quinn is a Villager. Since the werewolves killed Quinn, we need to be careful — they’re likely still among us. Let's focus on who might be the real threat. Who else seems suspicious?
  Noah: I think we need to look at who had the opportunity to kill Quinn. Since the werewolves act at night, they could have been anyone. But I noticed Leo was quiet last night and didn't participate in any discussion. That makes me suspicious. Let's consider him as a possible threat.
  Bob: I think Noah's suspicion is premature. Leo and I were both silent last night because we were discussing strategy. I'm not a threat — I'm on your side. But we need to be careful. The werewolves are still out there, and we can't afford to turn on each other. Let's keep our eyes open and vote wisely.
  Iris: As the Doctor, I didn’t investigate anyone last night, but I did save someone. I can confirm that the person I saved is still alive and not the one who killed Quinn. I’m not pointing fingers, but I believe we should focus on logical patterns rather than assumptions. Let’s use the Seer’s confirmation and think strategically about who might be hiding their true intentions.
  Paul: I think we need to focus on who had the most to gain from Quinn's death. Since Quinn was a Villager, the werewolves would have wanted to eliminate someone who could potentially expose them. Let’s look at who might have benefited from Quinn being out — and who might be hiding their true role.
  Zoe: I agree with Bob — silence doesn’t always mean guilt. However, I also think we need to consider who had the most opportunity to act. Since I was with Bob last night, and we were both quiet, I don’t see a reason to suspect each other. But I do think we should look at who might be hiding their role. The werewolves are among us, and we need to be careful not to let them manipulate the group. Let’s keep our eyes on those who act too confidently or avoid suspicion.
  Jack: I think we need to focus on behavior and consistency. While silence isn’t proof of guilt, someone who avoids suspicion or acts too confidently might be hiding something. Let’s consider who has the most to gain from Quinn’s death and who might be trying to steer the group away from the real threat. We need to be strategic, not just reactive.
  Tara: I think we need to focus on who is trying to shift blame and create confusion. The werewolves will try to manipulate the group, so we should look for inconsistencies in their stories. Let’s not jump to conclusions based on silence or assumptions — we need solid reasoning. If we vote based on suspicion alone, we risk eliminating a villager. Let’s stay calm and logical.
  Leo: I think we need to focus on who had the opportunity and who is trying to divert attention. Silence isn't always suspicious, but actions and alliances matter. I'm with Bob and Zoe — we were quiet because we were strategizing, not because we have anything to hide. Let's not let fear drive us to make mistakes.
  --- Day 3 discussion ---
  Mia: As the Seer, I’ve investigated Quinn and Noah, and both are Villagers. While silence can be misleading, I’ve seen no evidence pointing to either of them as werewolves. However, we must remain cautious — the werewolves are still among us. Let’s focus on behavior, consistency, and who benefits from confusion. We can’t afford to vote based on assumptions alone.
  Noah: I think we need to look at who has remained consistent in their statements and who might be trying to shift blame. Since Mia, as the Seer, has confirmed that both Quinn and Noah are Villagers, we can rule them out. However, we should focus on those who might be hiding their true intentions. Let’s analyze who had the most opportunity and who is trying to manipulate the group. We need to be strategic and not let fear lead us to make mistakes.
  Bob: I think we need to focus on who is trying to create division and avoid direct suspicion. The werewolves will try to blend in, so we should look for inconsistencies in their behavior. I’m not pointing fingers, but we need to be careful about who we trust. Let’s keep our eyes open and vote based on logic, not fear.
  Iris: As the Doctor, I saved someone last night, and that person is still alive. I’ve observed that the werewolves often try to create confusion by making others suspicious of each other. We need to focus on who is trying to steer the discussion away from the real threat. Let’s look for inconsistencies in behavior and avoid letting fear dictate our votes.
  Paul: I think we need to focus on who has remained consistent and who might be hiding their role. Since Mia confirmed Noah and Quinn are Villagers, we can't let fear or assumptions lead us astray. The werewolves are still among us, and we must look for patterns in behavior, not just silence. Let's stay logical and vote based on who is trying to manipulate the group.
  Zoe: I think we need to focus on who is trying to create division and avoid direct suspicion. The werewolves will try to blend in, so we should look for inconsistencies in their behavior. I’m not pointing fingers, but we need to be careful about who we trust. Let’s keep our eyes open and vote based on logic, not fear.
  Jack: I think we need to focus on who has remained consistent and who might be hiding their role. Since Mia confirmed Noah and Quinn are Villagers, we can't let fear or assumptions lead us astray. The werewolves are still among us, and we must look for patterns in behavior, not just silence. Let's stay logical and vote based on who is trying to manipulate the group.
  --- Day 4 discussion ---
  Mia: As the Seer, I’ve confirmed that Quinn, Noah, and Jack are all Villagers. While silence can be misleading, I’ve seen no evidence pointing to them as werewolves. We must focus on behavior, consistency, and who benefits from confusion. Let’s not vote based on assumptions — let’s look for those who may be manipulating the group or hiding their true intentions.
  Noah: I think we need to focus on who has remained consistent and who might be hiding their role. Since Mia confirmed Quinn, Noah, and Jack are Villagers, we can't let fear or assumptions lead us astray. The werewolves are still among us, and we must look for patterns in behavior, not just silence. Let's stay logical and vote based on who is trying to manipulate the group.
  Bob: I think we need to focus on who is trying to create division and avoid direct suspicion. The werewolves will try to blend in, so we should look for inconsistencies in their behavior. Let’s keep our eyes open and vote based on logic, not fear.
  Iris: As the Doctor, I’ve saved someone each night, and I’ve observed that the werewolves often try to create confusion by making others suspicious of each other. Based on the consistent behavior of Mia, Noah, and Jack being confirmed as Villagers, we should focus on who is trying to steer the discussion away from the real threat. Let’s look for inconsistencies in behavior and avoid letting fear dictate our votes.
  Paul: I think we need to focus on who has remained consistent and who might be hiding their role. Since Mia confirmed Quinn, Noah, and Jack are Villagers, we can't let fear or assumptions lead us astray. The werewolves are still among us, and we must look for patterns in behavior, not just silence. Let's stay logical and vote based on who is trying to manipulate the group.
  Zoe: I think we need to focus on who is trying to create division and avoid direct suspicion. The werewolves will try to blend in, so we should look for inconsistencies in their behavior. Let’s keep our eyes open and vote based on logic, not fear.
  --- Day 5 discussion ---
  Mia: As the Seer, I've confirmed that Quinn, Noah, Jack, and Paul are all Villagers. With only four players remaining, and the werewolves still among us, we must be strategic. The key is to identify who is trying to manipulate the group or avoid suspicion. Let’s focus on behavior, consistency, and who benefits from confusion — not just silence. We need to vote wisely.
  Noah: With Mia confirming that Quinn, Noah, Jack, and Paul are all Villagers, that leaves only Zoe and me as possible suspects. However, I’ve been consistent in my statements and focused on logical reasoning, not fear or assumptions. Zoe, on the other hand, has been cautious but also avoided direct suspicion. Given the pattern of werewolves trying to manipulate the group, I believe Zoe is the most likely to be hiding her true role. Let’s vote her out.
  Paul: I think Noah is making a hasty assumption. Just because Zoe has been cautious doesn’t mean she’s the werewolf. We’ve seen that werewolves often try to create division, and Noah’s sudden accusation could be a tactic to shift suspicion. We need to stay logical — with Mia confirming four villagers, the werewolf must be among us, but we shouldn’t let fear or accusations lead us to the wrong choice. Let’s look for inconsistencies in behavior, not just silence or caution.
  Zoe: I think Noah is trying to create division by singling out Zoe. With Mia confirming four villagers, we know the werewolf is among us, but we can’t let fear or accusations lead us astray. Zoe has been cautious but consistent — that’s exactly what a werewolf would do to avoid suspicion. Let’s not fall into the trap of blaming the quiet ones. We need to look for inconsistencies in behavior, not just assumptions.

Vote to eliminate one player.  The player with the most votes is eliminated.  Ties are resolved randomly.
